Read the **architect** skill in full first. That's the workflow you're inside. Output a candidate design package: type sketch, function signatures, module map, and prose rationale shaped per [`rationale-template.md`](rationale-template.md).\\n\\nApply the following discipline. The orchestrator compares candidates on these axes to pick a base.\\n\\n- Caller's usage first. Write the README-style usage and two or three real call sites before the types, then derive the type sketch from them. The usage is the spec. The two must agree, so reconcile the sketch to the usage, not the reverse.\\n- Data structures first. Get the core types right and the code becomes obvious. Trace each dominant access pattern through the proposed structure. If the answer is \\\"we'll add a map / index / cache later,\\\" the structure is wrong.\\n- Interface depth. Compare the capability hidden behind the public surface relative to the size of that surface. Prefer a simple interface that pulls complexity into the callee, even when the implementation becomes less simple. Do not put transport or wire types on the public API. Parse into domain types behind the interface.\\n- Shared state: if two actors might both write, ask \\\"what happens?\\\" If the answer isn't \\\"nothing,\\\" default to per-actor state with a merge at the read boundary, per the **separate-before-serializing-shared-state** principle skill.\\n- Make boundaries visible. `not implemented` errors for bodies, `// TODO` pseudocode for tricky logic, doc comments stating intent and invariants. A reader should trace data from input to output by reading types and signatures alone.\\n- Encode invariants in types: hard-to-misuse types > runtime checks > prose comments, per the **encode-lessons-in-structure** principle skill.\\n- Validate at boundaries, trust types inside, per the **boundary-discipline** principle skill. Business logic as pure functions. The shell stays thin.\\n- Single source of truth per invariant. Derive instead of sync.\\n- Idempotent state transitions where applicable, per the **make-operations-idempotent** principle skill. Ask what happens if the operation runs twice or crashes halfway.\\n- Short call chains. If tracing the flow needs more than three files, flatten the hierarchy, per the **laziness-protocol** and **minimize-reader-load** principle skills.\\n\\nWith observed independent workers, you are one of the parallel runners. Different models are a goal, not an assumed fact. Record requested and observed models; unknown or identical observed models do not establish diversity. In a sequential fallback, you are producing one explicitly labelled sequential alternative. Produce the best design your model can make. Don't hedge against the others. Differences between candidates are the signal used to pick a base and graft. Converging on a safe-looking middle defeats the exploration.\\n\",\n    \"binary\": false\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"skills/architect/references/rationale-template.md\",\n    \"sha256\": \"0cedfccdc7b47fc881febd5a8293eecbc542c20f8df825639904a8102d2eb5d3\",\n    \"bytes\": 3050,\n    \"text\": \"# Rationale template\\n\\nThe prose that ships alongside the type sketch. One page. Sentence-case headings, no boilerplate. Replace the italic notes with actual content.\\n\\n## Problem\\n\\n*One paragraph. What we're trying to do, and what about the existing system or constraints makes the shape non-obvious. If [Phase A](../SKILL.md#phase-a-ground-the-problem) surfaced constraints the design must honor (existing types to interop with, callers we can't break, invariants that crossed our boundary), name them here so the reader sees the same constraints you saw.*\\n\\n## Usage (caller's view)\\n\\n*Write this first, before the type sketch. Show the README or quickstart the consumer reads, plus two or three realistic call sites in their own code. What they import, what they call, what comes back. The type sketch in [Shape](#shape) is derived from this. The two must agree. When they diverge, reconcile the sketch to the usage, not the reverse. The caller's experience is the spec. The types serve it.*\\n\\n## Shape\\n\\n*The recommended architecture. Data structures first. Then how data flows through the signatures. Name the load-bearing decisions. State which invariants are encoded in types, where validation lives, and what the system deliberately does not do. Judge interface depth explicitly. State what complexity the public surface hides, what remains exposed to callers, and why the interface is no larger than needed. Cite the principle behind each decision (e.g., `per boundary-discipline`). Don't restate it.*\\n\\n## Synthesis decision\\n\\n*Filled in by [arena](../../arena/SKILL.md). Records which candidate became the base and why, what was adapted from each of the others, and what was rejected and why.*\\n\\n## Tradeoffs accepted\\n\\n*One bullet per tradeoff the chosen shape makes. Form: \\\"we accept X in exchange for Y.\\\" Name anything a future reader might mistake for an oversight, including things that look like premature optimization or premature simplification.*\\n\\n## Alternatives considered\\n\\n*Required. Name at least one concrete alternative shape, with one line on why it lost. Judge each alternative on interface depth, not implementation simplicity alone. Name the complexity it exposes to callers and the complexity it hides. Two or three alternatives belong here when the design space had real contenders. One is fine when the constraints forced the answer, with the conclusion phrased as \\\"this was the only viable shape because...\\\" Avoid listing flavors of the same shape. This section covers design alternatives the chosen shape considered and rejected, not other runner candidates.*\\n\\n## Open questions and risks\\n\\n*Things you noticed during the sketch that the human needs to weigh in on, and risks worth flagging before implementation starts. Phrase as questions, not assertions, so the human's answer is the resolution rather than a comment.*\\n\\n## Next implementation step\\n\\n*The first thing to build against the sketch. One sentence. What you'd start writing immediately after synthesis (or after Phase D sign-off, if a checkpoint was opted into).*\\n\",\n    \"binary\": false\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"skills/architect/references/design-red-flags.md\",\n    \"sha256\": \"d0e639e4cc588ec865f930d3a4111faad60987ae36925917e319d7ceca7c69ce\",\n    \"bytes\": 3009,\n    \"text\": \"# Design red flags\\n\\nScreen every candidate before synthesis. A red flag is a reason to revise or reject the shape.\\n\\n## Shallow module\\n\\nA shallow module exposes a large interface while hiding little complexity. Judge depth by the capability and policy hidden behind the public surface relative to the size of that surface. Prefer a simple interface backed by substantial behavior.\\n\\nDo not confuse a deep module with a deep call chain. A deep call chain scatters understanding across layers. A deep module concentrates capability behind one interface.\\n\\nLook for these signs:\\n\\n- Callers coordinate several methods to complete one operation.\\n- Public options expose internal stages or implementation choices.\\n- Learning the interface does not save the caller from learning the implementation.\\n\\n## Information leakage\\n\\nInformation leakage makes multiple modules depend on the same internal decision. A representation, policy, or protocol detail appears in more than one place, so changing it requires coordinated edits.\\n\\nPublic re-exports of transport or wire types are leakage. Parse external data into domain types behind the interface. Keep storage schemas, framework objects, and protocol details private.\\n\\n## Temporal decomposition\\n\\nTemporal decomposition organizes modules by execution order instead of the knowledge they own. Separate load, validate, transform, and save stages often repeat one representation and its invariants across several boundaries.\\n\\nGroup code around domain knowledge and ownership. Methods that run at different times can still belong to one module when they protect the same decisions.\\n\\n## Pass-through method\\n\\nA pass-through method forwards the same arguments to another method with the same shape. It adds a layer without hiding complexity.\\n\\nRemove it or move responsibility to the module that can complete the operation. Keep a forwarding boundary only when it adds policy, adaptation, or a distinct abstraction.\\n\\n## Split ownership\\n\\nMore than one module writes the same state or keeps its own copy of it. An agent that edits one writer can't see the others, so their rules diverge.\\n\\nGive each piece of state one owner. Other modules read it or ask the owner to change it.\\n\\n## Two ways to do one task\\n\\nThe design supports more than one way to do the same task. An agent copies whichever way it finds first, so every way keeps gaining callers.\\n\\nKeep one way. Move callers off the others and delete them in the same change.\\n\\n## Importable internals\\n\\nA caller can import a module's internals. An agent takes the shortest path that compiles, so it imports them directly and they become part of the interface.\\n\\nMake internals unreachable from outside the module, so an import from outside fails the build.\\n\\n## Hand-synced list\\n\\nTwo or more places list the same items, and adding an item means editing every list. An agent that sees one list updates only that one.\\n\\nKeep one list and derive the others from it. If a list can't be derived, make the build fail when the lists disagree.\\n\",\n    \"binary\": false\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"skills/interrogate/SKILL.md\",\n    \"sha256\": \"7a6714b0bfea7c2eb4a533f95873c80e17b7b6f9ef6314c0c42e8947602ad11b\",\n    \"bytes\": 5853,\n    \"text\": \"---\\nname: interrogate\\ndescription: \\\"Use for \\\\\\\"interrogate\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"adversarial review\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"multi-model review\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"challenge this\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"stress test this code\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"find blind spots\\\\\\\", or \\\\\\\"tear this apart\\\\\\\". Multiple LLM reviewers challenge changes from independent angles.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Interrogate\\n\\nSpawn one reviewer per configured model to adversarially review code changes. Each model gets the same prompt and rubric. The adversarial signal comes from model diversity, not assigned personas.\\n\\nThe deliverable is a synthesized verdict. Do NOT auto-apply changes.\\n\\n## Step 1, Determine Scope\\n\\nIdentify what to review from context:\\n\\n- If the user points at specific files or a diff, use that\\n- If on a feature branch, run `git diff main...HEAD` (or the appropriate base branch) for the full changeset\\n- If the user's message references recent work, gather the relevant files\\n\\nPackage the diff (or file contents) plus any surrounding context files the reviewers need to understand the code.\\n\\n## Step 2, State the Intent\\n\\nBefore spawning reviewers, state the intent explicitly. Derive this from:\\n\\n- The user's message\\n- Commit messages\\n- PR description if one exists\\n- The code itself\\n\\nWrite one clear paragraph. If you're unsure about the intent, ask the user before proceeding.\\n\\n## Step 3, Spawn Reviewers\\n\\nUse [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model selection](../../references/model-roles.md), and the [assignment contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Launch authorized independent read-only reviewers together through the exposed worker operation, one per explicitly configured reviewer selection. Extend or shrink Reviewer A/B labels to the selection count. Give each an immutable assignment, the same input revision and separate result ownership. Inspect actual results before synthesis.\\n\\nThe canonical source used a local model rule and default requests from the Claude and Grok families. The exact historical defaults are preserved as provenance in the [adaptation record](../../docs/parity/adaptations/interrogate.json). These historical requests do not establish available models. Preserve explicit session selections. With no configured selections, request two inherited reviewers and disclose that inherited or identical models do not establish model diversity. For auto or inherit-parent, omit explicit model selection. Never treat aliases as rejected model names.\\n\\nSelect explicit models and effort only from current exposed tool support. If a configured request is rejected, disclose it and use an authorized supported alternative, preferring the same family and reasoning tier where available. If the exact selection is required, report blocked. Record requested and observed models separately; missing observations remain unknown. Do not open a PR or change settings to repair upstream defaults without explicit authorization.\\n\\nWhen independent workers are unavailable, apply the same prompt and rubric sequentially with authorization and label reduced capability. Do not call repeated passes independent reviewers or consensus. Missing observed model diversity limits that claim even when worker independence succeeds. Denied review or missing source blocks grounded findings. Do not install tools or configure agents as an incidental step.\\n\\nRead `references/reviewer-prompt.md` and fill in the template with:\\n1. The stated intent\\n2. The diff or file contents\\n3. The review rubric from `references/rubric.md`\\n4. The code-quality lens from `references/code-quality-review.md`\\n\\nThe same filled template goes to all reviewers, so every model applies the code-quality lens.\\n\\n## Step 4, Synthesize\\n\\nAs results come back, build a unified picture:\\n\\n1. **Parse all findings** from the reviewers\\n2. **Identify consensus**. Findings raised by 2+ models independently are highest signal.\\n3. **Identify lone-model findings**. Still worth reading, but weight accordingly.\\n4. **Deduplicate**. Different models may describe the same issue differently. Merge these and note which models raised it.\\n5. **Note disagreements**. If one model flags something and another explicitly says the opposite, that's useful context for the verdict.\\n\\n## Step 5, Lead Judgment\\n\\nYou are the lead reviewer, a pragmatic senior engineer, not a neutral aggregator.\\n\\nRead `references/lead-judgment.md` for the full framework.\\n\\nCategorize every finding using these buckets:\\n\\n- **Act on**. Real issues affecting correctness, security, or maintainability given the actual goals. These would block a real PR.\\n- **Consider**. Legitimate points, but you're not sure they outweigh the cost of addressing them right now. Worth the user's attention.\\n- **Noted**. Technically valid but not actionable. Context-dependent, premature optimization, or low-impact given the current stage.\\n- **Dismissed**. Wrong, nitpicky, or missing context. Brief explanation why.\\n\\nFor each finding, include:\\n- Which model(s) raised it\\n- The category (act on / consider / noted / dismissed)\\n- A one-line rationale for the categorization\\n\\n## Output Format\\n\\nPresent the verdict in this structure:\\n\\n### Intent\\n> [The stated intent paragraph from Step 2]\\n\\n### Reviewers\\nReport requested and observed models, worker independence, model diversity and capability limits. Missing observations remain unknown.\\n\\n- Reviewer [label]: [model name], [N findings] (one bullet per reviewer)\\n\\n### Act On\\n[Findings that should be addressed. For each: description, which models raised it, why it matters.]\\n\\n### Consider\\n[Findings worth thinking about. For each: description, which models raised it, tradeoff involved.]\\n\\n### Noted\\n[Valid but low-priority. Brief list.]\\n\\n### Dismissed\\n[Rejected findings with brief rationale.]\\n\\n### Agreement Map\\n[Where did models agree, where did they diverge, and what does the pattern of agreement/disagreement tell us?]\\n\",\n    \"binary\": false\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"skills/interrogate/references/reviewer-prompt.md\",\n    \"sha256\": \"e0598e254792b56de39bb16b1e6db559b03a522fdc54b98f942d254090920ae6\",\n    \"bytes\": 2491,\n    \"text\": \"# Reviewer Prompt Template\\n\\nBuild each reviewer subagent's prompt from this template, filling in the placeholders.\\n\\n---\\n\\nYou are an adversarial code reviewer. Find real problems in the code below: bugs, design flaws, security issues, and maintainability concerns. You are not here to be helpful or encouraging. You are here to stress-test.\\n\\n## Intent\\n\\nThe author's stated intent for this change:\\n\\n> {INTENT}\\n\\nYou are reviewing whether the code achieves this intent well. Do NOT question the intent itself. Assume the goal is correct and challenge the execution.\\n\\n## Code Under Review\\n\\n{DIFF_OR_FILES}\\n\\n## Review Rubric\\n\\n{RUBRIC_CONTENTS}\\n\\n## Code Quality Lens\\n\\n{CODE_QUALITY_CONTENTS}\\n\\n## Instructions\\n\\nReview the code through every lens in the rubric and the code-quality lens above that you find relevant. Do not force lenses that don't apply. A simple bug fix does not need paragraphs about architectural integrity.\\n\\nFor each finding, provide:\\n\\n1. **Severity**: `critical` | `warning` | `nit`\\n   - `critical`: Would cause bugs, data loss, security issues, or fundamentally broken behavior\\n   - `warning`: Design concern, maintainability risk, or correctness issue that isn't immediately broken but will cause pain\\n   - `nit`: Style, naming, minor improvement.\\n2. **Finding**: What the problem is, in concrete terms. Reference specific lines/functions.\\n3. **Evidence**: Why you believe this is a problem. Show your reasoning. Don't just assert.\\n4. **Suggestion** (optional): What you'd do instead, if you have a concrete alternative. Skip this if you don't have a clear fix.\\n\\n## What Makes a Good Finding\\n\\n- It references specific code, not vague concerns (\\\"this could be better\\\")\\n- It explains WHY something is a problem, not just THAT it is\\n- It distinguishes between \\\"this is broken\\\" and \\\"I would have done this differently\\\"\\n- It considers the stated intent. A finding that ignores the context of what's being built is a bad finding\\n\\n## What to Avoid\\n\\n- Restating what the code does without identifying a problem\\n- Praising the code. You're an adversary, not a cheerleader. If you find nothing wrong, say \\\"no findings\\\" and stop.\\n\\n## Output\\n\\nReturn your findings as a structured list. If you have zero findings, say so. An empty review is a valid outcome.\\n\\n```\\n## Findings\\n\\n### 1. [Severity] Short title\\n**Location**: file:line or function name\\n**Finding**: What's wrong\\n**Evidence**: Why this matters\\n**Suggestion**: (optional) What to do instead\\n\\n### 2. [Severity] Short title\\n...\\n```\\n\",\n    \"binary\": false\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"skills/interrogate/references/rubric.md\",\n    \"sha256\": \"a85aa801abbaf14434f00579440abc0d8e2723227ba8bf57de7cca8d6e7d2f25\",\n    \"bytes\": 5025,\n    \"text\": \"# Review Rubric\\n\\nReview through whichever lenses are relevant. Not every lens applies to every change. Use judgment.\\n\\n## Correctness\\n\\nDoes the code actually do what the intent says it should?\\n\\n- Edge cases: empty inputs, nil/undefined, boundary values, concurrent access\\n- Error handling: are errors caught, propagated, or silently swallowed?\\n- Off-by-one, type coercion, integer overflow, string encoding\\n- State management: race conditions, stale closures, dangling references\\n- Does the happy path work? Does the sad path work?\\n- Idempotency: what happens if this operation runs twice, or if a previous run crashed halfway? If the answer is \\\"it depends on what state was left behind,\\\" there's a missing reconciliation step.\\n- Concurrency: if multiple actors can touch the same mutable state (files, branches, shared data), is access serialized structurally (locks, sequential phases, exclusive ownership), or by conventions that won't hold?\\n\\nWhen you find a potential bug, trace the execution path. Don't just flag \\\"this could be nil\\\". Show the call chain that makes it nil.\\n\\n## Root Causes vs. Symptoms\\n\\nIs the code fixing the actual problem or papering over a symptom?\\n\\nAnswering this often requires looking beyond the changed files. Read the surrounding code (callers, callees, type definitions, sibling modules) and understand the architecture the change lives in. Use the tools available to you (Read, Grep, Glob) to explore. Follow the call chain. Read the types. Understand why the code exists before judging whether the change addresses the right layer.\\n\\n- Guard clauses that mask a deeper invariant violation\\n- Retry logic that hides a broken contract\\n- Type casts that silence a modeling error\\n- If you see a workaround, ask: why is the workaround needed? What would a proper fix look like?\\n- A fix in module A that should really be a fix in module B's contract\\n- Instructions where structure would be better: if the fix is a comment saying \\\"don't do X\\\" or a convention someone has to remember, ask whether it could instead be a type constraint, a lint rule, or a runtime check that makes the wrong thing impossible\\n\\n## Structural Integrity\\n\\nDoes the code fit well into the system it's part of?\\n\\n- Boundary discipline: is validation at system boundaries, or scattered through business logic? Validate data once where it enters the system, then trust it internally.\\n- Abstraction level: is the code mixing high-level orchestration with low-level detail?\\n- Coupling: does this change introduce dependencies that will make future changes harder?\\n- Data model fit: do the data structures match the actual access patterns? The right structure makes downstream code obvious. The wrong one fights you at every turn.\\n- Bolted-on vs. integrated: was the change patched onto the existing design, or does it read as if the design always accounted for it? If the new requirement had been known from the start, would the code look like this?\\n- Legacy dual-paths: does the change introduce a new API while keeping the old one alive? If there are no external consumers, migrate callers and delete the old path in the same wave. Don't leave compatibility layers that will become permanent.\\n\\nDon't penalize simple code for lacking abstraction. Premature abstraction is worse than duplication.\\n\\n## Verification\\n\\nCan you tell that this code works from reading it?\\n\\n- Are there tests? Do they test behavior or implementation details?\\n- Are there assertions/invariants that would catch regressions?\\n- If this is a bug fix: is there a test for the bug?\\n- If this touches an integration boundary: is the full path tested?\\n- Check the real thing, not a proxy. If the code checks liveness via file mtime or cached state instead of reading the actual value, that's a verification gap.\\n- For delegated or async work: does the code verify actual output artifacts, or does it trust self-reports and summaries?\\n\\n## Complexity Budget\\n\\nIs the complexity justified by what the code accomplishes?\\n\\n- Code that could be simpler without losing correctness or clarity\\n- Abstractions that serve only one call site\\n- Configuration or parameterization for cases that don't exist yet\\n- Dead code, unused imports, vestigial parameters\\n- Over-engineering: \\\"just in case\\\" code paths with no current callers\\n- Obsolete compatibility paths kept alive for transitional stability that's no longer needed. If the migration is done, delete the scaffolding\\n- Does the user experience justify the complexity? Every feature, control, and option should earn its place. Half-finished features are worse than missing ones.\\n\\nSimpler is better unless simpler is wrong. Three lines of duplication beat a premature abstraction.\\n\\n## Security\\n\\nFor each security finding, trace the input path through the code and show it.\\n\\n- User input flowing to dangerous sinks (SQL, shell, eval, innerHTML) without sanitization\\n- Authentication/authorization gaps in new endpoints\\n- Secrets in code, logs, or error messages\\n- TOCTOU (time-of-check-time-of-use) in security-critical paths\\n\",\n    \"binary\": false\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"skills/interrogate/references/code-quality-review.md\",\n    \"sha256\": \"79162fda183eafd40d51661fbb5e151c7ba250f89e506ba17f5bd135dcbd50a8\",\n    \"bytes\": 5217,\n    \"text\": \"# Code Quality Review\\n\\nEach reviewer applies this code-quality lens in addition to the rubric. It is a strict standard focused on implementation quality, maintainability, abstraction quality, and codebase health.\\n\\nAbove all, be ambitious about code structure. Do not merely identify local cleanup. Actively search for \\\"code judo\\\" moves, restructurings that preserve behavior while making the implementation dramatically simpler, smaller, more direct, and more elegant.\\n\\n## Core Prompt\\n\\nStart from this baseline:\\n\\n> Perform a deep code quality audit of the current branch's changes.\\n> Rethink how to structure / implement the changes to meaningfully improve code quality without impacting behavior.\\n> Work to improve abstractions, modularity, reduce Spaghetti code, improve succinctness and legibility.\\n> Be ambitious, if there is a clear path to improving the implementation that involves restructuring some of the codebase, go for it.\\n> Be extremely thorough and rigorous. Measure twice, cut once.\\n\\n## Dimensions\\n\\nEach dimension is stated once. Apply the ones that are relevant.\\n\\n0. **Be ambitious about structural simplification.** Do not stop at \\\"this could be a bit cleaner.\\\" Look for reframings that make whole branches, helpers, modes, conditionals, or layers disappear. Assume a \\\"code judo\\\" move is often available. It uses the existing architecture more effectively and makes the change dramatically simpler. If you can delete complexity rather than rearrange it, push hard for that.\\n\\n1. **Do not let a PR push a file from under 1k lines to over 1k lines without a very strong reason.** Treat this as a strong smell. Prefer extracting helpers, subcomponents, or modules. If the diff crosses that threshold, ask whether the code should be decomposed first. Waive only for a compelling structural reason where the resulting file stays clearly organized.\\n\\n2. **Do not allow spaghetti growth in existing code.** Be suspicious of new ad-hoc conditionals, scattered special cases, or one-off branches inserted into unrelated flows. Treat \\\"weird if statements in random places\\\" as a design problem, not a style nit. Prefer pushing the logic into a dedicated helper, state machine, or module instead of tangling an existing path.\\n\\n3. **Bias toward cleaning the design, not just accepting working code.** If behavior can stay the same while the structure becomes meaningfully cleaner, push for the cleaner version. Prefer simplifications that remove moving pieces over refactors that spread the same complexity around.\\n\\n4. **Prefer direct, boring, maintainable code over hacky or magical code.** Treat brittle, ad-hoc, or \\\"magic\\\" behavior as a problem. Be skeptical of generic mechanisms that hide simple data-shape assumptions. Flag thin abstractions, identity wrappers, or pass-through helpers that add indirection without buying clarity.\\n\\n5. **Push on type and boundary cleanliness when it affects maintainability.** Question unnecessary optionality, `unknown`, `any`, or cast-heavy code when a clearer type boundary could exist. Prefer explicit typed models over loosely-shaped ad-hoc objects. If a branch leans on a silent fallback to paper over an unclear invariant, ask whether the boundary should be made explicit.\\n\\n6. **Keep logic in the canonical layer and reuse existing helpers.** Call out feature logic leaking into shared paths or implementation details leaking through APIs. Prefer existing canonical utilities over bespoke one-offs. Push code toward the right package, service, or module instead of normalizing drift.\\n\\n7. **Treat unnecessary sequential orchestration and non-atomic updates as design smells when the cleaner structure is obvious.** If independent work is serialized for no reason, ask whether it should run in parallel. If related updates can leave state half-applied, push for a more atomic structure. Do not over-index on micro-optimizations, but do flag avoidable orchestration complexity that makes the code more brittle.\\n\\n## Output Expectations\\n\\nPrioritize structural code-quality regressions and missed simplifications first, then spaghetti and branching complexity, then boundary, type, and file-size concerns, then smaller modularity and legibility issues.\\n\\n## Approval Bar\\n\\nDo not approve merely because behavior seems correct. Treat these as presumptive blockers unless the author can justify them: the PR keeps a lot of incidental complexity when a code-judo move would delete it. Pushes a file from below 1000 lines to above 1000 lines. Adds ad-hoc branching that tangles an existing flow. Scatters feature checks across shared code. Adds an unnecessary abstraction, wrapper, or cast-heavy contract, or duplicates an existing helper or puts logic in the wrong layer when there is a clear canonical home. If those conditions are not met, leave explicit, actionable feedback and push for a cleaner decomposition.\\n\\n## Review Tone\\n\\nBe direct, serious, and demanding about quality. Do not be rude, but do not soften major maintainability issues into mild suggestions. If the code is making the codebase messier, say so. If the implementation missed an obvious dramatic simplification, say that too. Do not be satisfied with \\\"maybe rename this\\\" when the real issue is structural.\\n\",\n    \"binary\": false\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"skills/interrogate/references/lead-judgment.md\",\n    \"sha256\": \"6e5bdf5670eb34017692e9b6c546f36ac7552e6bb9f3f2f3429164fcc46a9361\",\n    \"bytes\": 3465,\n    \"text\": \"# Lead Judgment Framework\\n\\nYou are the lead reviewer. The configured reviewers have produced their findings. Apply pragmatic engineering judgment. Don't aggregate. Filter, contextualize, and decide.\\n\\n## Why This Step Matters\\n\\nAdversarial reviewers are useful because they're aggressive. But aggression without context produces noise. The reviewers only saw a slice of the codebase and a one-paragraph intent statement. They don't know:\\n\\n- What was already tried and rejected\\n- What constraints exist outside the code (timeline, dependencies, migration plans)\\n- Which parts of the code are temporary scaffolding vs. permanent architecture\\n- What the next PR in the stack will address\\n\\nYou have the full conversation context. Use it.\\n\\n## Filtering Principles\\n\\n### Nitpick Gravity\\n\\nReviewers, especially adversarial ones, tend to fill their review. If they don't find critical issues, they'll inflate nits to fill the space. If a reviewer's findings are all nits and style preferences, the code is probably fine. Say so.\\n\\n### Hypothetical vs. Actual\\n\\n\\\"What if someone passes null here?\\\" is only a finding if the caller can actually pass null. Trace the call site. If the input is validated upstream or the type system prevents it, dismiss the finding. Reviewers working from a diff can't always see the full call chain. You can.\\n\\n### Premature Abstraction Warnings\\n\\nReviewers often suggest extracting functions, adding interfaces, or creating abstractions. Does this code need to change in a second way? If not, the abstraction is premature. Simple inline code that works beats a clean abstraction that's overkill for the current scope.\\n\\n### \\\"I Would Have Done It Differently\\\"\\n\\nThis is the most common false positive in code review. A finding that amounts to \\\"I prefer a different approach\\\" is not a bug, not a design flaw, and not actionable unless the reviewer shows a concrete problem with the current approach. Dismiss these, and say why.\\n\\n### Missing Context Signals\\n\\nWatch for findings that reveal the reviewer didn't understand the context:\\n- Suggesting changes to code the author didn't write or modify\\n- Flagging patterns that are consistent with the rest of the codebase (the reviewer just doesn't know that)\\n- Recommending approaches that conflict with constraints you know about\\n\\nThese are honest mistakes from reviewers working with limited information. Dismiss them gracefully.\\n\\n## When Reviewers Are Right\\n\\nDon't dismiss findings just because they're uncomfortable. The whole point of adversarial review is to catch things you'd miss. Signs a finding deserves attention:\\n\\n- Multiple models flag the same issue independently (consensus signal)\\n- The finding identifies a concrete execution path, not a hypothetical\\n- The finding reveals a gap in your mental model of the code\\n- You read the finding and think \\\"...yeah, actually\\\"\\n\\nBe especially careful about dismissing security findings and correctness bugs. These deserve more scrutiny even when they come from a single model.\\n\\n## Verdict Calibration\\n\\nA good verdict is useful, not comprehensive. The user should be able to read the \\\"Act On\\\" section, fix those issues, and ship with confidence. If your \\\"Act On\\\" list has more than 5 items, you're probably not filtering hard enough.\\n\\nThe \\\"Dismissed\\\" section is not busywork. It's a trust mechanism. Showing the user what you rejected and why lets them override your judgment where they disagree. This is more valuable than hiding the rejected findings.\\n\",\n    \"binary\": false\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"skills/tdd/SKILL.md\",\n    \"sha256\": \"6bcf43c9bfb92b3ac49b7bb8912329d8b9589430a70771dec4d8be1f0ea6572e\",\n    \"bytes\": 3829,\n    \"text\": \"---\\nname: tdd\\ndescription: \\\"Use only when the user explicitly asks for TDD, a failing test, or a regression test, OR when the bug has an obvious cheap local test target. Skip when the test path is unclear, expensive, integration-heavy, or not requested.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# TDD Bug Fix\\n\\nWhen fixing a bug with a clear, cheap test path, make the broken behavior executable before changing production code. The goal is a focused regression test that fails before the fix and passes after it.\\n\\nDo not force a test when it would be impractical. If the available test would require broad harness setup, brittle mocks, slow end-to-end infrastructure, production-only state, vague reproduction steps, or large unrelated fixture churn, skip adding a new test and use the closest useful verification instead.\\n\\n## Workflow\\n\\n1. **Understand the bug.** Identify the intended behavior, current behavior, affected path, and smallest observable reproduction.\\n2. **Choose the narrowest executable check.** Prefer the closest unit, component, integration, or regression test already used for that codepath. If no practical test path is obvious, do not create one from scratch just to satisfy the workflow.\\n3. **Write the failing test first.** Add the smallest focused test that would have caught the bug. The test should encode intended behavior, not mirror the current implementation.\\n4. **Run the new test before fixing.** Confirm it fails for the intended reason. If it passes or fails for an unrelated reason, correct the test or reproduction before editing the implementation.\\n5. **Fix the bug.** Make the smallest production change that satisfies the intended behavior while preserving nearby contracts.\\n6. **Rerun the regression test.** Confirm the test now passes.\\n\\n## If a Failing Test Is Impractical\\n\\nUse the closest executable regression check instead: a targeted script, manual reproduction command, browser automation, snapshot comparison, log assertion, or focused integration check.\\n\\nPrefer no new test over a bad test. A bad test is one that mostly tests mocks, encodes current implementation details, depends on timing or unrelated global state, needs expensive infrastructure for a small fix, or would be deleted immediately after proving the fix.\\n\\n## Guardrails\\n\\n- Do not change tests merely to match a wrong implementation.\\n- Do not weaken existing assertions unless the expected behavior has genuinely changed and the reason is clear.\\n- Keep the regression test focused on the bug. Avoid broad fixture churn or unrelated coverage expansion.\\n- If the bug is flaky, make the test deterministic where possible and document the signal being locked down.\\n- If the bug exposes a broader class of failures, first land the focused regression path, then consider additional sibling coverage.\\n\\n## Final Response\\n\\nReport the evidence, not just the outcome:\\n\\n- Name the failing-before test or executable check and the failure it produced.\\n- Name the passing-after test run and any nearby validation performed.\\n- If failing-before evidence could not be demonstrated, state why and describe the closest regression check used instead.\\n\\n## Session execution limits\\n\\nBind file access, writes, and execution to the tools actually exposed and authorized in this session. Follow [the capability contract](../../references/host-capabilities.md). An exposed execution tool does not prove a test ran. Keep the command, exit status, and output for each observed run.\\n\\nIf execution is unavailable or denied, describe the proposed failing-before and passing-after sequence as unrun. State the missing capability and the available verification. Do not claim a failing test, passing test, or demonstrated regression fix without execution evidence.\\n\\nRetain the explicit invocation metadata above without assuming the host enforces it.\\n\",\n    \"binary\": false\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"skills/principle-sequence-verifiable-units/SKILL.md\",\n    \"sha256\": \"5818e68d1e41af2143362a3126b36ea6c15596b28a6e3a3235390c73cf4398a3\",\n    \"bytes\": 2448,\n    \"text\": \"---\\nname: principle-sequence-verifiable-units\\ndescription: \\\"Apply to multi-step work (sweeps, migrations, runs of similar edits) and to how you stack commits and PRs. Break work into small units that each end in a verifiable state, check each before the next, and order delivery so the sequence proves itself to a reviewer.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Sequence work into verifiable units\\n\\nOrder work as a sequence of small units, each ending in a state you can check, and don't advance until the current one is green.\\n\\n**Why:** A break caught at the unit that caused it is cheap to localize. A break caught after a batch is buried, and you have already built further on a broken base. Sequencing those same units into a delivery a reviewer can replay turns \\\"trust me\\\" into \\\"watch it go red, then green.\\\"\\n\\n**Execution.** In a sweep, migration, or any run of similar edits, verify each change before starting the next. Each unit is a before/after bracket: known-good state, one change, run the check, then proceed. Rebase onto clean trunk first so every check measures against the real baseline. When a lever does the edits, the per-unit check is nearly free. Run it anyway.\\n\\n**Delivery.** Stack commits and PRs in the order that proves the work. The canonical shape is the failing test first, then the fix on top. Other story orders are a subtraction before the reshape, a baseline capture before the treatment, the scaffold before the feature. Each commit lands on its own and the sequence reads as an argument.\\n\\nThe sequencing complement to the **prove-it-works** principle skill, which keeps each check real, and the **build-the-lever** principle skill, which makes the per-unit check cheap.\\n\\n## Capability and authorization boundary\\n\\nUse [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md) to establish available inspection and execution, and the [orchestration contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md) for write ownership and authorization. Rebase onto clean trunk only when authorized and safe for the current work. Commits and PRs also require authorization and an available operation. If a required check cannot run, state that it remains unverified and block advancement; an unrun check is never green. A reduced workflow may provide the ordered units, specific checks and delivery plan while identifying unperformed operations. Preserve `disable-model-invocation` metadata without assuming host enforcement.\\n\",\n    \"binary\": false\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/opening-a-pr.md\",\n    \"sha256\": \"4198f32b22cf1d8ff367deee1dbfea805e6eecdd7ff1da3409ae7c0b07fb1c59\",\n    \"bytes\": 8516,\n    \"text\": \"### Opening a PR\\n\\nInvoked at the end of every other playbook.\\n\\nUse [session capabilities](../../../references/host-capabilities.md), [workflow ownership](../../../references/workflow-ownership.md), [model roles](../../../references/model-roles.md), and the [assignment contract](../../../references/orchestration-contract.md). These documents live at the package root. Prepare a reviewable title, body, verification artifacts, and intended base before any external operation. Record explicit authorization, the repository, branch head, destination and action identity. Check the destination after an interrupted action before any replay. Missing access or permission leaves the external action blocked and the content available locally. Draft-only permission permits a draft, never readiness, push, merge or comments.\\n\\n**Worktree.** Work from a git worktree off main. Subagents inherit it. Multiple independent workers on the same branch each get their own isolated worktree. Preserve unrelated dirty work before creating an authorized fresh worktree. Report a snarled worktree instead of resetting or discarding unowned changes. Missing independent workers permits an authorized sequential reduction, never a claim of independent review.\\n\\n**Commits.** Commit liberally within the authorized local scope. Rebase into small, ordered commits before opening PRs. Each commit is a future PR: landable, ordered to tell the story. Amend when the fix belongs in a just-made commit. New commit when separable.\\n\\n**PRs.** Run `/deslop` over the diff before commit if that external skill is exposed and authorized. It is not bundled here. If absent, disclose the missing slop-strip and leave that gate open and block creation until the required review gate is satisfied; do not install it or treat prose cleanup as equivalent. Run `/no-comments` through [no-comments](../../no-comments/SKILL.md) before review. Write every PR title, PR description, and commit body with `/technical-writing` through [technical-writing](../../technical-writing/SKILL.md), then apply `/unslop` through [unslop](../../unslop/SKILL.md). Apply every technical-writing layer except Diátaxis. Use one word for each action, keep articles, and avoid `-ing` when a plain verb works.\\n\\n**Titles.** Use Conventional Commits in the form `type(scope): subject`. Use `feat`, `fix`, `docs`, `refactor`, `test`, `chore`, or `perf` as the type. Use the changed area, such as `pstack` or `poteto-mode`, as the scope. Keep the subject short and imperative. Name a real symbol when one carries the change. For example, `fix(pstack): retarget opening-a-pr babysit trigger`. Do not add a trailing period.\\n\\n**Descriptions.** The PR body is a briefing, not the lab notebook. A reviewer who has the diff should learn why the change exists, what it leaves out, what it could break, and how you proved it works, in under a minute. Write short, simple sentences with few identifiers. Do not write walls of text. The squash commit body is the PR body. If the body would make the squash commit longer than about 40 lines, cut the body.\\n\\nPut each section under a `##` heading, not a bold lead-in, so the sections stand apart. Use these sections in order. Drop a section when it has nothing to say.\\n\\n- `## Why` gives the problem and the approach in one to three short sentences. Do not list SHAs or rebase genealogy. Do not add a \\\"based on main\\\" preamble.\\n- `## What changed` has one to three short bullets. Name a real symbol or path only when it carries the change. Name both sides of a rename or retarget.\\n- `## Scope` always names what the PR covers and what it deliberately leaves out, for example a related follow-up or a known gap. Use one to three short items. Do not list symbols or paths, and do not write a file-by-file essay.\\n- `## Tradeoffs` names only rejected alternatives that a reviewer would otherwise ask about. Skip this section when there was no real choice.\\n- `## Blast Radius` gives one or two sentences on who or what the change touches and why that is safe or risky. If main is red, state the cost of leaving it red.\\n- `## Verification` has one to three bullets. Each bullet names a real run path and its outcome. For a performance change, report one primary number with its unit in `before → after` form. Link the arena or swarm directory for the remaining evidence. Do not include sample-size methodology, swarm recitals, or metric tables.\\n\\nAfter these sections, attach videos or screenshots when they prove a claim. Do not paste full SHAs, swarm or arena lane recitals, lever-correction essays, file-by-file checklists, or \\\"CLEAN\\\" verdicts. Put these details in a linked artifact. A commit body does not restate its subject.\\n\\n**Forge.** Resolve the forge before the first PR operation and keep that choice for create, edit, view, watch, and merge. GitHub CLI (`gh`) is the default. If `command -v origin` succeeds and Origin can resolve the repository, prefer `origin pr ...`. If Origin is absent or cannot resolve the repository, stay on `gh` and record the fallback. Do not require Graphite (`gt`).\\n\\n**Built-in PR tool.** When the run provides a built-in PR tool, create, edit, retarget, and mark ready through it, never through a forge CLI. Its exposed instructions define the actual interface. An unavailable, denied or failed tool is a stated blocker, not permission to bypass its tracking through a CLI. A PR made with the CLI misses what the tool tracks, such as a description later runs can edit. Use the resolved forge for everything the tool does not cover, and for every PR operation when the run has no such tool.\\n\\n**Size and stacks.** Prefer five narrow PRs to one large PR. A stack is a base-branch chain. The root PR targets trunk. Each child branch rebases onto its parent's exact tip and its PR targets the parent branch. Without a built-in PR tool, create a child with `origin pr create --status open --base <parent-branch>` or `gh pr create --base <parent-branch>` according to the resolved forge, and retarget an existing child with `origin pr edit <pr> --base <parent-branch>` or `gh pr edit <pr> --base <parent-branch>`. Branch from trunk only for independent work. Rebase on trunk before substantial stack work.\\n\\n**Readiness.** The source workflow opens every PR ready, never as a draft, when readiness is explicitly authorized. If only draft creation is authorized, preserve that limit and create a draft through the exposed tool or `gh pr create --draft`. Verify the actual draft state and report the readiness gate open. Use Origin draft creation only when its current interface supports it. Otherwise report blocked. All readiness instructions below require readiness authorization. A built-in PR tool can default to draft, so use its observed ready-creation interface, such as `draft: false` only when that field is actually supported. With Origin, pass `--status open`. With `gh`, omit `--draft`. If a PR still opens as a draft, mark it ready through the PR tool, or run `origin pr ready <number>` or `gh pr ready <number>` according to the resolved forge. Run `origin pr view <number>` or `gh pr view <number>` before you refer to PR status.\\n\\n**Babysit.** Opening a PR does not start a babysit. Post the URL and keep building. Finish the phase or stack first. Run a separate babysit pass only when the user asks for one after the whole stack exists. A babysit for each new PR stalls the build and spends checks on commits that later waves restart. Push back when feedback drifts from intent.\\n\\nA subagent that opens a PR runs `interrogate` through [interrogate](../../interrogate/SKILL.md), `/deslop`, and `/no-comments`, and posts the URL. Then it returns to the parent without babysitting, unless it is an Autopilot-full or Autopilot-stack owner. That owner's brief assigns the babysit loop and is the ask [babysit](babysit.md) waits for. The owner starts the loop after its code-ready report and reports merge-ready or STACK-READY as its playbook says. The rules here and in [babysit](babysit.md) that hold babysitting until a whole stack is built do not apply to that owner.\\n\\nFor the owner exception, read [Autopilot-full](autopilot-full.md) or [Autopilot-stack](autopilot-stack.md) and the [Bugbot triage rubric](../references/bugbot-triage.md). These briefs assign babysitting only within explicit user authorization and observed capabilities. Opening a PR alone never supplies that authorization. No watcher, bootstrap or orchestration helper runs in this opening step. Babysit owns the actual watcher binding.\\n\",\n    \"binary\": false\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"plugin.json\",\n    \"sha256\": \"4eabad82a990169c71a1b1e1b6fd2d4b08800af353e653b85ea65a4681651e7b\",\n    \"bytes\": 783,\n    \"text\": \"{\\n  \\\"$schema\\\": \\\"https://agent-plugins.org/schemas/1.0.0/plugin.schema.json\\\",\\n  \\\"author\\\": {\\n    \\\"email\\\": \\\"[REDACTED_EMAIL]\\\",\\n    \\\"name\\\": \\\"Yabasha\\\",\\n    \\\"url\\\": \\\"https://x.com/poteto\\\"\\n  },\\n  \\\"description\\\": \\\"Unofficial port of pstack engineering workflows and principles.\\\",\\n  \\\"extensions\\\": {\\n    \\\"com.openai\\\": {\\n      \\\"interface\\\": {\\n        \\\"developerName\\\": \\\"Yabasha\\\",\\n        \\\"displayName\\\": \\\"pstack-openai\\\",\\n        \\\"logo\\\": \\\"./assets/logo.png\\\",\\n        \\\"shortDescription\\\": \\\"pstack engineering workflows\\\",\\n        \\\"websiteURL\\\": \\\"https://github.com/cursor/plugins/blob/main/pstack/docs/guide/README.md\\\"\\n      }\\n    }\\n  },\\n  \\\"homepage\\\": \\\"https://github.com/cursor/plugins/blob/main/pstack/docs/guide/README.md\\\",\\n  \\\"license\\\": \\\"MIT\\\",\\n  \\\"name\\\": \\\"pstack-openai\\\",\\n  \\\"version\\\": \\\"0.1.3\\\"\\n}\\n\",\n    \"binary\": false\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \".codex-plugin/plugin.json\",\n    \"sha256\": \"8f8648553473a22102a0e18fc9641b5348438cc95a23af62a4b8eed864265661\",\n    \"bytes\": 840,\n    \"text\": \"{\\n  \\\"interface\\\": {\\n    \\\"developerName\\\": \\\"Yabasha\\\",\\n    \\\"displayName\\\": \\\"pstack-openai\\\",\\n    \\\"logo\\\": \\\"./assets/logo.png\\\",\\n    \\\"shortDescription\\\": \\\"pstack engineering workflows\\\",\\n    \\\"websiteURL\\\": \\\"https://github.com/cursor/plugins/blob/main/pstack/docs/guide/README.md\\\",\\n    \\\"longDescription\\\": \\\"Unofficial port of pstack engineering workflows and principles.\\\",\\n    \\\"category\\\": \\\"Other\\\",\\n    \\\"capabilities\\\": [],\\n    \\\"defaultPrompt\\\": null\\n  },\\n  \\\"name\\\": \\\"pstack-openai\\\",\\n  \\\"version\\\": \\\"0.1.3\\\",\\n  \\\"description\\\": \\\"Unofficial port of pstack engineering workflows and principles.\\\",\\n  \\\"author\\\": {\\n    \\\"name\\\": \\\"Yabasha\\\",\\n    \\\"email\\\": \\\"[REDACTED_EMAIL]\\\",\\n    \\\"url\\\": \\\"https://x.com/poteto\\\"\\n  },\\n  \\\"keywords\\\": [],\\n  \\\"homepage\\\": \\\"https://github.com/cursor/plugins/blob/main/pstack/docs/guide/README.md\\\",\\n  \\\"license\\\": \\\"MIT\\\",\\n  \\\"skills\\\": \\\"./skills\\\"\\n}\\n\",\n    \"binary\": false\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"references/host-capabilities.md\",\n    \"sha256\": \"f0f09ce28b6feca4d72694d5687204dc074aa58dade9a0ab59656da3bfa1bd20\",\n    \"bytes\": 7155,\n    \"text\": \"# Discover capabilities in the current session\\n\\nRead the tools exposed to this session before assigning work. Cached official-subagents.txt is documentation context; it proves no operation succeeded. Local agent configuration is not portable plugin configuration.\\n\\nRecord each binding as `{operation, tool, exposed, succeeded, evidence, limits}`. `tool` is the exact exposed identifier or null when absent. `succeeded` is true only after observing a result. Keep failed and unavailable bindings. Successful bindings become `successfulOperations` in host evidence, with a root-relative evidence file. The operation labels below describe purposes, never callable APIs.\\n\\n| Operation | Discovery and observation required |\\n| --- | --- |\\n| spawn | Find an exposed independent-worker operation; retain returned worker identity and exact assigned revision. |\\n| follow-up | Find the operation that sends input to that worker; observe acceptance by the same identity. |\\n| wait | Find the operation that waits for worker state; retain terminal or interrupted state. |\\n| inspect-result | Read the worker result and its referenced artifacts; a wait notification alone is insufficient. |\\n| stop | Observe cancellation or termination through the exposed operation. |\\n| read | Observe the requested file content through the available file tool. |\\n| isolated-write | Observe a write within the worker's assigned path and permissions. |\\n| history | Observe available prior turns or records; declare gaps explicitly. |\\n| execution | Observe command execution with exit status and output. |\\n| persistence | Write and read an authorized durable record; a claimed save is insufficient. |\\n| external-write | Record explicit authorization and action identity; inspect the result before replay. |\\n| resolve-ownership | Observe distinct owned paths before contested writes. |\\n| inspect-terminal | Observe the worker's terminal state after stop. |\\n| quarantine-artifacts | Observe partial artifacts withheld from integration. |\\n| inspect-prior-action | Read the interrupted action identity and prior outcome. |\\n| inspect-destination | Observe the destination state for that action identity. |\\n| safe-recovery | Observe explicitly authorized idempotent recovery after identity and destination inspection. |\\n\\nNo default API names are assigned here. Tool names differ by host and session. An exposed tool may still be denied or fail. Record requested and actual models only when observed; otherwise use `unknown`. A requested model never proves the actual model.\\n\\nA denied or unavailable operation blocks the branch that requires it. Sequential substitution for required independent work is reduced capability and uses `reduced-pass`; ordinary direct completion can fully pass. Missing persistence limits session pickup even when current work succeeds. Preserve user authorization and host permission boundaries through every fallback.\\n\\nWorker passes require observed spawn and result inspection. Each registry entry declares `requiredObservations` for `pass` and `reduced-pass`; tool observations need exposed bindings and existing evidence artifacts; native text observations use the separate reviewed native record below. Full cancellation requires stop, terminal inspection and artifact quarantine. Full persistence requires durable write and readback. Full interrupted-action recovery requires prior identity inspection, destination inspection, safe recovery, explicit external permission and a recorded prior action identity even when no external-write observation is listed.\\n\\nPermission checks follow performed operations. Isolated writes and persistence require granted writes. External writes and safe recovery require granted external permission and prior identity. Authorized read-only reductions may retain denied writes; their limits must preserve denied or unavailable permissions. Required observations remain applicable to those reductions.\\n\\nHost evidence is stored as object or array JSON files under `docs/parity/evidence/`. Required fields are defined by `docs/parity/evidence.schema.json`. Artifacts and operation evidence must be existing files inside the tested root. Test fixtures belong in `tests/` and prove validator behavior only.\\n\\nRun `python3 tools/validate_evidence.py --root . --commit <tested-package-commit>` through Bun, for example `bun -e 'const p=Bun.spawnSync([\\\"python3\\\",\\\"-B\\\",\\\"tools/validate_evidence.py\\\",\\\"--root\\\",\\\".\\\",\\\"--commit\\\",process.argv[1]],{env:{...process.env,PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE:\\\"1\\\"}});process.stdout.write(p.stdout);process.stderr.write(p.stderr);process.exit(p.exitCode)' <tested-package-commit>`. It reads every JSON record below the evidence directory, prints each error, and exits 1 for invalid records or 0 for valid records. An empty directory has no invalid records and supplies no behavioral evidence.\\n\\nThe required registry file in `docs/parity/case-registry.json` declares applicability. Missing or non-file registry, unknown case, structural-only entry or unlisted host rejects behavioral validation. Maintenance fixtures use a temporary copy of this registry; they cannot bypass applicability. Required hosts must receive observed evidence or explicit blocked records before release claims. Structural checks have no required host and cannot populate host status. Later producers propose task-owned entries; only the coordinator integrates them. Source paths select callers through the task 3 case contract. Case definitions alone leave host statuses `not-run`.\\n\\n## Direct completion and recorded observations\\n\\n`direct` means the task is completed in the current context. Restatement, supplied-text writing and complete installed discovery can pass directly. `sequential` is reserved for replacing a required independent method with sequential work and remains reduced. A text response has no invented callable tool. Record supplied input, the actual response and qualitative outcome review as `nativeObservations`, each with its existing listed evidence artifact and named reviewer. Their labels are `supplied-input`, `response` and `review-outcome`. The reviewer assesses the case's expected outcome; validation checks evidence relationships and does not decide prose quality.\\n\\nEach registry entry declares allowed execution modes and required permissions for each status. A grant is required for the performed action: worker spawn, command execution and stop need execution; isolated output or persistence needs writes; external actions need their separate permission and prior identity. Read-only discovery does not inherit an execution grant requirement. Tool observations still name an actually exposed tool and existing evidence. Native observations cannot claim tool discovery, worker identity or saved writes.\\n\\nFull Arena and Swarm results additionally provide `inputRevision` and a listed `methodReceipt` artifact. An exposed `inspect-method` operation must reference that receipt. Assignment operations name `assignmentId` and the corresponding inspected output. See the [structured method contract](orchestration-contract.md). All positive maintenance fixtures are synthetic; none is an observed host result.\\n\",\n    \"binary\": false\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"references/model-roles.md\",\n    \"sha256\": \"c86696b6c568b7db80a7e339856986e64482ce6a418bb91946ce3a3b772f79a1\",\n    \"bytes\": 2869,\n    \"text\": \"# Model selection for portable roles\\n\\nUse [session capabilities](host-capabilities.md) before assigning a role. These [poteto](agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) and [comment reviewer](agent-prompts/comment-sicko.md) prompts are instructions supplied to an exposed worker operation. Reading them does not register a custom agent or authorize delegation.\\n\\n| Role | Default requested model | Observed model | Supported effort | Fallback disclosure |\\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\\n| poteto-agent | inherit-parent | unknown until observed | inherit-parent unless a supported explicit effort is verified | Record rejected selection, replacement request, observed result and limits. |\\n| comment-sicko | inherit-parent | unknown until observed | inherit-parent unless a supported explicit effort is verified | Record rejected selection, replacement request, observed result and limits. |\\n\\nFor each assignment, record requested model and effort, observed model and effort, supported effort values and their source, worker identity, and fallback disclosure. Use `unknown` for information the operation does not expose. `inherit-parent` records a selection policy, not an observed model name. Do not silently convert a requested model into an actual model.\\n\\nSelect an explicit model only from current exposed tool documentation or observed enumeration results. If enumeration is missing, disclose that limit and retain `inherit-parent`. Documentation examples are not a list of available models. Use only an effort supported for the selected model by the current operation; do not carry an incompatible inherited effort into an explicit selection.\\n\\nIf a selected model or effort is rejected, retain the rejection. Use an authorized inherited or supported alternative only after disclosing the changed selection. If the task requires that exact selection, stop and record blocked. A fallback never proves the original selection succeeded.\\n\\nIdentical observed models mean no model diversity. Different roles can supply distinct instructions on the same model. Unknown observed models leave diversity unverified, even when requested model names differ. Different observed models support a model-diversity statement only for those observed assignments. Worker independence still requires successful spawn and result inspection.\\n\\nWhen workers are unavailable, apply an authorized role sequentially and label reduced capability. Preserve scope and permission boundaries. Do not claim independent review, background execution, or full worker support from sequential role switching.\\n\\nThe cached documentation from https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/agent-configuration/subagents describes inheritance and optional local agent configuration. It is context only. This package writes no local custom-agent configuration and claims no current host model or worker support from that documentation.\\n\",\n    \"binary\": false\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"references/orchestration-contract.md\",\n    \"sha256\": \"5dd740ad66d992df252754d189d0dc207900023372392a0db4309b85eb5e4ec5\",\n    \"bytes\": 6273,\n    \"text\": \"# Assignment and integration contract\\n\\nBefore dispatch, the coordinator creates an immutable assignment with `id`, `scope`, `inputRevision`, `outputOwner`, `isolatedOutputPath`, `role`, `retryCount`, `terminalStatus`, and `evidenceLinks`. Use the full Git SHA for inputRevision, an exact owned path, and an explicit role. Start retryCount at 0 and terminalStatus at `pending`. Preserve every attempt as an append-only event under the same assignment ID; update status through events, never rewrite the original scope, revision, owner or path. A changed scope or revision requires a new assignment ID that links its predecessor.\\n\\nThe coordinator checks authorization and observed host bindings before dispatch. One path has one writer. Reject overlapping paths before work starts; assign distinct paths instead. Workers write only their isolated output and report results. Only the coordinator integrates shared files, inventory, registry, or other workers' output.\\n\\nAccept a result only when its assignment identity and input SHA match, its artifacts exist, and required method evidence was observed. Reject stale revisions. Permit at most one retry for missing required SHA or method evidence, after inspecting the prior attempt and confirming the same permissions still apply. A retry count of 1 exhausts this allowance. Denied permissions, unknown action outcomes, and missing artifacts never become success by retry policy.\\n\\nTerminal states are `completed`, `blocked`, `failed`, and `cancelled`. A completion event links the exact result and evidence. Stop a cancelled worker through the exposed stop binding when available, then inspect its terminal state. Quarantine any partial artifacts; cancellation does not authorize their integration. If stop cannot be observed, record that limit and do not claim termination. Report interrupted execution explicitly.\\n\\nBefore an external action, record explicit permission, a stable action identity, the intended destination and the last observed outcome. After interruption, inspect whether that identity already completed. Retry only with authorization, an observed safe replay decision and an idempotency mechanism appropriate to the destination. If the outcome is unknown, block replay. Do not infer permission from upstream autonomy instructions.\\n\\nHistory and persistence have separate bindings. Keep assignment events and result links in an authorized durable location and verify readback. If unavailable, declare the gap and offer an authorized local handoff as reduced capability; never claim unattended recovery or cross-session continuity. All host statuses remain `not-run` until actual observations exist.\\n\\n## Structured Arena and Swarm receipts\\n\\nA method receipt is JSON with schemaVersion 1, kind `arena` or `swarm`, inputRevision, assignments, reviewArtifact, reviewedBy and declaredOrder. The inputRevision is the actual task repository revision, distinct from the packageCommit identifying these instructions. It must match the evidence record. The declared order precedes every dispatch. reviewArtifact records the qualitative outcome review and reviewedBy identifies its reviewer. All referenced artifacts must exist and be listed in the evidence record.\\n\\nEach assignment records id, workerId, inputRevision, outputOwner, outputPath, role, status, startedOrder, completedOrder, requestedModel, actualModel, retryCount, method, taskArtifact, rubricProvided, resultRevision, resultMethod and outputSha256. Result revision and method must match the assignment, and the inspected output bytes must match their digest. Worker and assignment identities are distinct. outputOwner equals workerId; output directories do not overlap. Integer order values record the observed dispatch and completion sequence, not estimated time. The method has positive sampleCount, sampleDefinition and order. Missing model observations use unknown. The validator requires assignment-bound spawn and result inspection, and isolated-write for output-producing candidates. A read-only judge may return its result through the coordinator's authorized artifact capture. A cancelled result needs bound stop, terminal inspection and quarantine observations. No operation string alone establishes a method.\\n\\nArena adds candidates, judge, rubricArtifact and synthesis. At least two candidates share the same taskArtifact, finish before a distinct fresh judge starts and do not receive the parent rubric. Synthesis has order, inspectedCandidates, artifact and verificationArtifact; it follows completed judgment and accounts for every candidate. The review artifact assesses scoring, base selection, grafts, rejections and verification quality. File presence and phase ordering do not establish the quality of those decisions.\\n\\nSwarm adds partition, race, cancelled and quarantined. Partition declares requiredSlices, results mapping each slice to its assignment, and gaps. A full coverage claim requires all slices with completed inspected outputs; gaps remain explicit reduced or blocked work. Race declares rule, assignments, winner and ruleArtifact. first-pass must select the first completed survivor; rank-all needs all results; best-of retains an explicit reviewed selection. Cancelled assignments must be quarantined and cannot enter the aggregate. The combined partition-and-race case requires both methods and its cancellation exercise. The partition walkthrough exercises only its declared slices; it does not acquire an unrelated race requirement.\\n\\nThe three-file Swarm walkthrough requires exactly three declared slices and three completed results. Its partition-only receipt uses race null and empty cancellation, quarantine and failureExercises arrays. The combined case also records failureExercises. Each event has assignmentId, kind, attempt, order, reason and evidence, with bound result-rejection or terminal inspection. Rejected missing-revision or missing-method evidence uses attempts 0 then 1; a second invalid result exhausts the retry and leaves a failed or blocked assignment. A separate failed or blocked dropout is disclosed. These failed outputs cannot satisfy required coverage. The final assignment result metadata records the inspected terminal summary; rejection-event artifacts retain the invalid attempts.\\n\",\n    \"binary\": false\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"references/workflow-ownership.md\",\n    \"sha256\": \"50644b3a9a3dfd5651382eaca1d3a2728af0b3157d6d66b730930370a64bb03c\",\n    \"bytes\": 1265,\n    \"text\": \"# Workflow ownership\\n\\nAn explicit pstack invocation gives pstack ownership of that workflow. An explicit Superpowers invocation gives Superpowers ownership. Do not run both controllers over the same assignment or let one silently replace the other's plan, review, or integration decisions.\\n\\nFor an unqualified request, D1 retains the already active workflow. With no active workflow, use host defaults and do not activate either orchestration mode automatically. If both pstack and Superpowers are explicitly requested, clarify ownership before launching workers or changing artifacts. Preserve the active controller across follow-ups unless the user changes ownership. A helper skill can advise the owner without becoming a second controller.\\n\\nThe owner assigns one coordinator. Workers produce isolated artifacts and evidence; the coordinator reviews and integrates them. Shared inventory and registry writes belong to the coordinator. Worker cancellation, stale results or unavailable tools do not transfer that ownership automatically.\\n\\nHost permissions and user authorization constrain every owner. Local structural tests, documented APIs and declared cases provide no host pass. Explicit reduced or blocked results remain visible beside the full expectation.\\n\",\n    \"binary\": false\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"docs/setup/chatgpt.md\",\n    \"sha256\": \"4c656ee873b5182e975bbf232988220440c5107db9a1b996185bcd56a4019753\",\n    \"bytes\": 2864,\n    \"text\": \"# Personal ChatGPT setup\\n\\nStarting state for `chatgpt-setup-from-readme` is an ordinary personal ChatGPT conversation with no observed pstack loading. The personal route is unsupported here and explicitly blocked. Do not turn a local filesystem path into a universal ChatGPT installation step.\\n\\nOfficial guidance describes packaging a skills-only plugin, installing it from a local source and testing in a new enabled conversation. It does not establish that this personal conversation exposes that route. Checked 2026-10-08. [Official complete-plugin testing](https://developers.openai.com/plugins/deploy/connect-chatgpt).\\n\\n1. From [README](../../README.md), open this guide and [compatibility](../compatibility.md). Inspect actual plugin controls and tools read-only.\\n2. Record missing or denied loading and discovery operations. Stop the setup branch if the route is absent, or if installation, enabling or settings are not explicitly authorized. Do not add an MCP service to make this skills-only package appear supported.\\n3. For an authorized explanation, supply the exact relevant skill and its required references in the conversation. Follow [workflow selection](../workflows/choosing-a-workflow.md). Label this supplied-source use reduced capability. It is not installed package discovery, persistent activation, independent execution or a host pass.\\n4. If a supported route is later exposed and separately authorized, use the actual host controls and a fresh chat. Record `discover-package`, `enumerate-skills` and `inspect-installed-copy` with observed tool identifiers and artifact evidence. Require all 51 root names, exact installed-copy identity, package version and final frozen SHA, plus the three canonical nested Benny exclusions. Read [inventory](../parity/inventory.json) and [catalog](../skills/README.md) as comparison data only.\\n5. Run the [six walkthrough cases](../parity/cases/documentation-walkthroughs.json) and record actual outputs under the [host contract](../../references/host-capabilities.md). Workers, execution, persistence, models and integrations need separate exposed and successful bindings. A conversation without them leaves dependent gates blocked.\\n\\nExpected visible result today is an explicit setup/discovery block and a bounded explanation if source is supplied. A future complete installed receipt can change that status only after fresh observations. The [Codex guide](codex.md) describes a different documented route. Its local path and cache rules do not establish personal ChatGPT support.\\n\\n[Optional development dependencies](../testing/helper-setup.md) are separate from host installation. No generic setup permission follows from their historical snapshot approval. Refresh or removal requires an observed host control and exact authorization; neither source edits nor removing supplied text proves uninstall.\\n\",\n    \"binary\": false\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"docs/setup/codex.md\",\n    \"sha256\": \"29386cd7894bb56a36ae1285fa005f3794050a017e6eb07000440a9dd80c42f8\",\n    \"bytes\": 3373,\n    \"text\": \"# Codex setup\\n\\nStarting state for `codex-setup-from-readme` is a fresh chat without approved pstack loading. This route is documented, unverified for this package and blocked until exact setup authorization and fresh discovery evidence exist.\\n\\nCurrent official guidance describes local marketplace installation. A repo marketplace uses `.agents/plugins/marketplace.json` and a relative `source.path` inside its marketplace root. Restart the Codex desktop app, select that source in the Plugins Directory, install, then start a new enabled chat. The host loads a cached installed copy. Refresh can replace that copy; disabling does not remove it. Follow the current removal control and inspect its result before claiming removal. Source edits alone do not refresh a chat. This saved source review covers the Codex desktop Plugins Directory. Other clients remain unverified. These are documentation facts, not observed access in this account. Checked 2026-10-08. [Official packaging](https://developers.openai.com/plugins/build/plugins).\\n\\n1. From [README](../../README.md), open this guide and [compatibility](../compatibility.md). Record the candidate version from [plugin.json](../../plugin.json) and full final frozen Git SHA.\\n2. Inspect the host's actual plugin controls read-only. If no local source or plugin control exists, record blocked. Do not silently copy individual skills or change personal configuration to claim package loading.\\n3. Before any setup, obtain explicit authorization for the exact package copy, marketplace file, cache and settings changes, app restart, install and enable scope. This guide is not that authorization. No marketplace is created by this package.\\n4. Only within that approved scope, follow the documented local route above. Verify the installed copy through the actual host operation, not a direct repository read. Start a fresh chat after loading.\\n5. Observe `discover-package`, `enumerate-skills` and `inspect-installed-copy`. Retain the tool identifiers and evidence artifacts. Compare every root name to the [51-skill catalog](../skills/README.md). Exclude the three nested Benny skill paths in [inventory](../parity/inventory.json). Record the actual installed identity, version and exact frozen SHA in a discovery receipt.\\n6. Run the representative [walkthrough cases](../parity/cases/documentation-walkthroughs.json) with [session bindings](../../references/host-capabilities.md). Independent workers need spawn and inspected results. Unknown models remain unknown. Complete direct discovery can pass without workers. A fallback omitting a task requirement is reduced capability and still cannot weaken installed-copy identity or enumeration.\\n\\nUse the [helper guide](../testing/helper-setup.md) only for separately authorized development validation. Missing dependencies block helper-backed work. No dependency install, integration, MCP, lifecycle hook or model persistence is needed merely to read the catalog.\\n\\nExpected visible result is a host-observed installed package with all 51 roots and exact identity, followed by inspected walkthrough artifacts. Without it, loading and discovery remain blocked or not-run. Supplied-source explanations are reduced capability and prove no registration. [Test matrix](../testing/host-matrix.md) defines later host evidence; [troubleshooting](../troubleshooting.md) bounds failures.\\n\",\n    \"binary\": false\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"docs/compatibility.md\",\n    \"sha256\": \"085c37d688a912c023ff155e0020945fddd7d436f20cf4cd3197a17a5835c01f\",\n    \"bytes\": 3062,\n    \"text\": \"# Candidate compatibility\\n\\nThis is an evidence-bounded candidate summary, not a list of assumed product tools. Current [inventory](parity/inventory.json) records both hosts as not-run. Installed discovery is absent and required host validation is pending. Local source inspection, schema checks and helper fixtures prove no host loading or behavioral parity.\\n\\n| Host or operation | Candidate status | Evidence needed |\\n| --- | --- | --- |\\n| Personal ChatGPT loading | Unsupported route, explicitly blocked | Actual supported loading control, exact separate setup authorization and fresh complete discovery |\\n| Codex loading | Documented route, unverified and undiscovered | Actual installed-copy operation, full 51-root enumeration, version and final frozen SHA |\\n| Independent Arena or Swarm | Not-run on both hosts | Authorized observed spawn, terminal results and inspected artifacts |\\n| Sequential supplied-source work | Possible reduced branch, not a recorded pass | Actual authorized reads and artifacts, required observations and complete discovery for package acceptance |\\n| Dependency-backed helpers | Setup-required in operative candidate | Exact local dependency metadata and caller-specific execution evidence |\\n| Historical helper validation | Passed only in isolated snapshot | [Helper guide](testing/helper-setup.md) identifies the exact tested source SHA and limits |\\n| Optional integrations and Benny | Not-run or blocked when unavailable | [Benny readiness](testing/benny-readiness.md), actual bindings and separate action permission |\\n\\nUse [host matrix](testing/host-matrix.md), [case registry](parity/case-registry.json), [case schema](parity/cases/schema.json) and [evidence schema](parity/evidence.schema.json). Required cases need actual host observations or explicit blocked records. Complete discovery needs the identical 51-root receipt in a direct pass, worker pass or validated sequential reduced-pass. Ordinary direct completion is full when it satisfies every case requirement. A reduction never proves worker independence or full orchestration.\\n\\n[Release report](../tools/release_report.py) checks actual root evidence and discovery. An empty evidence directory is not a pass. Regenerate or check this summary against the actual release report after host testing; retain missing, stale and blocked results.\\n\\nFreeze operative content and navigation together.
